Um, let's see... I guess it's been a while now. Maybe around nine months? 

It kind of started when, well, when the loan on my condo started moving toward foreclosure. I know that's a pretty stressful thing for anyone to go through, so I really thought the worry would just fade away once I got used to the situation, you know? But it just sort of stuck around.

Um, well... I'd really love to say there are parts of my day where I just completely relax, because I know I should be able to do that. I try really hard to just focus on the positives. But, honestly, it feels like the worry is kind of... everywhere right now? I hope that doesn't sound too dramatic or anything.

And from there it just kind of snowballs into, you know, how I'll ever save money or just... move forward with my life in general. 

I really want to be the kind of person who can just leave work at work and feel at ease at home, but my brain just keeps bouncing from one worry to the next. I'm trying my best to keep it under control, I promise. Is it normal for it to jump around to so many different things like that? I hope I'm answering your question right, I'm sorry if I'm rambling.

Um... I guess I'd say it's... well, it's definitely more than just a mild annoyance. I really hate to admit this, because I always try to be so dependable, but... it's actually been causing me quite a bit of trouble lately. I hope you don't think less of me for saying that.

Even when everything is completely fine and I'm just sitting at my desk, or, you know, trying to enjoy my dance night. \n\nI try not to make a big deal out of it, but... does that make sense? Is that the sort of thing people usually come in here for?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a sudden episode where you felt intense fear or discomfort that reached a peak within minutes?\n\nPatient: Oh, um, no, I don't think so. I haven't really had a sudden spike like that. It's never an intense rush that just hits me all at once or anything. It's more of a... a constant, low-level hum in the background, if that makes sense? \n\nI definitely wouldn't want to make it sound worse than it actually is, so I'd have to say no to that one. I hope it's still okay to be here even if I don't have those really intense episodes? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you're worried about when you feel wound up?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wish it was just one specific thing, because then maybe it wouldn't be so bothersome? I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... my worry just kind of jumps around a lot. \n\nLike, it used to mostly just be about my condo, which I think is a pretty normal thing to stress over, right? But it doesn't really stay on the condo anymore. It'll jump from, um, worrying about whether my cousin is secretly upset with me, to suddenly being so anxious that I'll make a mistake on a support call at work. And then before I know it, I'm stressing over how I'll ever save enough money or, you know, move forward with my life. \n\nI know everyone worries about their jobs and their future, so I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. I'm sure it's probably just regular life stuff. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this constant, low-level anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... I guess it's been a while now. Maybe around nine months? \n\nIt kind of started when, well, when the loan on my condo started moving toward foreclosure. I know that's a pretty stressful thing for anyone to go through, so I really thought the worry would just fade away once I got used to the situation, you know? But it just sort of stuck around. \n\nI really hope I'm not overreacting by still feeling this way after nine months. I mean, people deal with housing issues all the time and handle it way better than I am. Does nine months sound like a long time for this kind of thing, or is that pretty normal?\n\nDoctor: Has this constant feeling of anxiety been affecting your ability to do daily activities or enjoy things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try not to let it get in the way, but... well, at work, I've kind of been falling behind on my support tickets lately. I just get so worried about making a mistake that I end up double-checking even the really simple tasks over and over. I feel terrible about it because my coworkers have had to pick up some of the slack, and I hate being a burden to them. I'm sure they're probably annoyed with me, even if they don't say it.\n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I usually love going to dance nights and I do some mentoring, but I've actually skipped several of them recently. I just feel so... overwhelmed, I guess, that I can't bring myself to show up. \n\nI know I should just push through it, and I feel really guilty for dropping the ball on things. I'm trying to do better, I promise. Do you think it's really bad that it's affecting my work and my hobbies like this? I hope I don't sound like I'm just making excuses for being lazy.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety mostly focused on social situations, like what others think of you, or is it more about everyday life and tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess it's a little bit of both? But maybe more about just... everyday life stuff, mostly. \n\nLike I mentioned, I definitely worry about things like my condo and my support tickets at work, and just generally trying to keep my life on track. I mean, I do worry about what people think of me, too—like my cousin or my coworkers—but it's usually tied to me feeling like I'm messing up or not doing a good enough job, rather than just being in a social situation itself, if that makes sense? \n\nI really try to be a good friend and a good employee, so I just get so anxious that I'm letting people down. I hope that's a normal way to feel. Does that sound like what you were asking? I'm not totally sure if I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety been affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little? But I really try hard not to let it. \n\nI mean, I don't want to be a downer or anything, so I try to keep it to myself when I'm around my friends or my cousin. I don't want them to think I'm, you know, constantly complaining or that I can't handle my own life. So I try to just put on a smile and be supportive of whatever they have going on. \n\nI guess I haven't been reaching out quite as much lately, just because I've been feeling so tired from worrying all the time. But I really hope they don't think I'm ignoring them! I'd hate for them to feel like I don't care about them. I'm sure I'm just overthinking it, right? I mean, everyone gets a little distant sometimes when they're stressed, don't they? I really hope I'm not being a bad friend.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're anxious in most areas of your life, or are there certain parts of your daily routine where you feel more at ease?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'd really love to say there are parts of my day where I just completely relax, because I know I should be able to do that. I try really hard to just focus on the positives. But, honestly, it feels like the worry is kind of... everywhere right now? I'm trying my best to keep it under control, I promise. Is it normal for it to jump around to so many different things like that? I haven't really had a sudden spike like that. It's never an intense rush that just hits me all at once or anything. It's more of a... a constant, low-level hum in the background, if that makes sense? \n\nI definitely wouldn't want to make it sound worse than it actually is, so I'd have to say no to that one. I hope it's still okay to be here even if I don't have those really intense episodes? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Is there something in particular that you're worried about when you feel wound up?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wish it was just one specific thing, because then maybe it wouldn't be so bothersome? I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... my worry just kind of jumps around a lot. \n\nLike, it used to mostly just be about my condo, which I think is a pretty normal thing to stress over, right? But it doesn't really stay on the condo anymore. It'll jump from, um, worrying about whether my cousin is secretly upset with me, to suddenly being so anxious that I'll make a mistake on a support call at work. And then before I know it, I'm stressing over how I'll ever save enough money or, you know, move forward with my life. \n\nI know everyone worries about their jobs and their future, so I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. I'm sure it's probably just regular life stuff. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this constant, low-level anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... I guess it's been a while now. Maybe around nine months? \n\nIt kind of started when, well, when the loan on my condo started moving toward foreclosure. I know that's a pretty stressful thing for anyone to go through, so I really thought the worry would just fade away once I got used to the situation, you know? But it just sort of stuck around. \n\nI really hope I'm not overreacting by still feeling this way after nine months. I mean, people deal with housing issues all the time and handle it way better than I am. Does nine months sound like a long time for this kind of thing, or is that pretty normal?\n\nDoctor: Has this constant feeling of anxiety been affecting your ability to do daily activities or enjoy things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try not to let it get in the way, but... well, at work, I've kind of been falling behind on my support tickets lately. I just get so worried about making a mistake that I end up double-checking even the really simple tasks over and over. I feel terrible about it because my coworkers have had to pick up some of the slack, and I hate being a burden to them. I'm sure they're probably annoyed with me, even if they don't say it.\n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I usually love going to dance nights and I do some mentoring, but I've actually skipped several of them recently. I just feel so... overwhelmed, I guess, that I can't bring myself to show up. \n\nI know I should just push through it, and I feel really guilty for dropping the ball on things. I'm trying to do better, I promise. Do you think it's really bad that it's affecting my work and my hobbies like this? I hope I don't sound like I'm just making excuses for being lazy.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety mostly focused on social situations, like what others think of you, or is it more about everyday life and tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess it's a little bit of both? But maybe more about just... everyday life stuff, mostly. \n\nLike I mentioned, I definitely worry about things like my condo and my support tickets at work, and just generally trying to keep my life on track. I mean, I do worry about what people think of me, too—like my cousin or my coworkers—but it's usually tied to me feeling like I'm messing up or not doing a good enough job, rather than just being in a social situation itself, if that makes sense? \n\nI really try to be a good friend and a good employee, so I just get so anxious that I'm letting people down. I hope that's a normal way to feel. Does that sound like what you were asking? I'm not totally sure if I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety been affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little? But I really try hard not to let it. \n\nI mean, I don't want to be a downer or anything, so I try to keep it to myself when I'm around my friends or my cousin. I don't want them to think I'm, you know, constantly complaining or that I can't handle my own life. So I try to just put on a smile and be supportive of whatever they have going on. \n\nI guess I haven't been reaching out quite as much lately, just because I've been feeling so tired from worrying all the time. But I really hope they don't think I'm ignoring them! I'd hate for them to feel like I don't care about them. I'm sure I'm just overthinking it, right? I mean, everyone gets a little distant sometimes when they're stressed, don't they? I really hope I'm not being a bad friend.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're anxious in most areas of your life, or are there certain parts of your daily routine where you feel more at ease?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'd really love to say there are parts of my day where I just completely relax, because I know I should be able to do that. I try really hard to just focus on the positives. But, honestly, it feels like the worry is kind of... everywhere right now? I really try to be there for people, but I just feel so drained. Does that make sense? I'm really trying to push through it, I promise, but it's just been really hard to manage everything right now.\n\nDoctor: Is there any part of your life where you feel completely free from worry or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'd really love to say yes. I mean, I try so hard to find those little moments of peace, you know? Like maybe when I'm just sitting on the couch with a cup of tea, I tell myself, \"Okay, just relax now.\" \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound like I'm just not trying hard enough... I don't think there's anywhere that's totally free from it right now. Even when I'm supposed to be relaxing, my brain is just sort of humming in the background, reminding me of all the things I should be doing or worrying about. \n\nI really wish I could just switch it off, I promise I'm trying. Is it bad that I can't find a place where it just stops? I hope I'm not a lost cause or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, or feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um, no... Even when I'm supposed to be relaxing, my brain is just sort of humming in the background, reminding me of all the things I should be doing or worrying about. \n\nI really wish I could just switch it off, I promise I'm trying. Is it bad that I can't find a place where it just stops? I hope I'm not a lost cause or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, or feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um, no... I don't think I experience anything quite like that. I mean, I know I mentioned double-checking my support tickets earlier, but that's really just me wanting to be thorough so I don't let my team down, you know? It's not like a... a ritual or a specific routine I feel forced to do. \n\nAnd I don't really get strange mental images stuck in my head or anything. It's honestly just the regular worrying about everyday things, like I was saying before. Is... is that what you meant? I just want to make sure I'm understanding you right and giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Is there any one area of your life where you feel your anxiety is significantly less than in others?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm really trying to think if there is. I want to give you a good answer, but... honestly, I don't think there's really any one area that's significantly better than the rest. \n\nI mean, I try my best to leave work stress at work and not bring it home, and I really do try to just enjoy my weekends... but the worry just kind of follows me everywhere, I guess. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um... I think we might have just talked about this a little bit, but... no, I really don't have anything like that. I wish I did! I'd love to tell you I have this perfect hobby or, you know, a specific routine where I just feel totally calm.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess my sleep hasn't been the greatest lately. I mean, I really do try to have good habits and get enough rest, I know how important that is! But... most nights, I get into bed just completely exhausted, and then my brain just kind of... turns on. 

It keeps replaying all these things, like loan notices, or work tickets, or just worrying about details I might have missed during the day. It's a little embarrassing to admit because I know I should just go to sleep, but sometimes I'm just lying there until two or three in the morning. And even when I finally do fall asleep, I usually wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. 

I hope it doesn't sound like I'm just whining about being tired... is that what you were asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right details.

I mean, I do worry about what people think of me, too—like my cousin or my coworkers—but it's usually tied to me feeling like I'm messing up or not doing a good enough job, rather than just being in a social situation itself, if that makes sense? \n\nI really try to be a good friend and a good employee, so I just get so anxious that I'm letting people down. I hope that's a normal way to feel. Does that sound like what you were asking? I'm not totally sure if I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety been affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little? But I really try hard not to let it. \n\nI mean, I don't want to be a downer or anything, so I try to keep it to myself when I'm around my friends or my cousin. I mean, everyone gets a little distant sometimes when they're stressed, don't they? I really hope I'm not being a bad friend.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're anxious in most areas of your life, or are there certain parts of your daily routine where you feel more at ease?\n\nPatient: Um, well... Like, one minute I'll be worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, and then suddenly I'm stressing over whether I'll make a mistake on a support call. And from there it just kind of snowballs into, you know, how I'll ever save money or just... move forward with my life in general. \n\nI really want to be the kind of person who can just leave work at work and feel at ease at home, but my brain just keeps bouncing from one worry to the next. I'm trying my best to keep it under control, I promise. Is it normal for it to jump around to so many different things like that? I hope I'm answering your question right, I'm sorry if I'm rambling.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety is causing you a lot of distress or is it more of a mild annoyance?\n\nPatient: Um... People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I guess we did just touch on this a little bit, didn't we? I really hate to repeat myself, I'm sorry! But, um... yes, I suppose it has. 

I mean, I really try to keep everything together, I promise I'm usually very dependable! But... like I mentioned, I've been so caught up in making sure I don't mess anything up at work that I'm checking and re-checking even the easiest things. And because of that, I've been falling behind on my support tickets, and... well, my coworkers have had to step in and help me out, which I feel just awful about. I hate being a burden to them.

And then outside of work, um... I've skipped a few of my dance nights, and even some mentoring sessions, which I usually love doing. I just... I get to the end of the day and I feel so overwhelmed that I just can't bring myself to go. 

I'm really trying to get better about it, though! I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or being lazy. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of what's going on.

I mean, everyone gets a little distant sometimes when they're stressed, don't they? I hope I'm answering your question right, I'm sorry if I'm rambling.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety is causing you a lot of distress or is it more of a mild annoyance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I'd say it's... well, it's definitely more than just a mild annoyance. I've actually skipped a few of my dance nights recently, and even some of my mentoring sessions. I just... I get so overwhelmed by the end of the day that I can't bring myself to show up. I really try to be there for people, but I just feel so drained. Does that make sense? I'm really trying to push through it, I promise, but it's just been really hard to manage everything right now.\n\nDoctor: Is there any part of your life where you feel completely free from worry or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'd really love to say yes. I mean, I try so hard to find those little moments of peace, you know? I don't think there's anywhere that's totally free from it right now. I hope I'm not a lost cause or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, or feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um, no... Um... yeah, I guess it really is everywhere right now. I'm sorry, I feel like I'm repeating myself again, I just... I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. 

But, um, yes. Like I was saying, it doesn't just stay on the condo and the loan stuff anymore. I really wish it did, because then I could just focus on fixing that! But instead, it just... it jumps to everything.

I don't know, I'll just be trying to sleep and my brain will start spiraling about how I'll ever save enough money or just... move forward with my life at all. 

It feels like no matter where I am or what I'm doing, there's always something I'm stressing over. I really try to keep a positive attitude about things, I promise I'm not usually this much of a worrier! It's just... lately, it feels like it's taken over a bit. Does that answer what you were asking? I hope I'm not rambling too much.

I hope that's a normal way to feel. Does that sound like what you were asking? I don't want them to think I'm, you know, constantly complaining or that I can't handle my own life. I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but... my worry just kind of jumps around a lot. \n\nLike, it used to mostly just be about my condo, which I think is a pretty normal thing to stress over, right? But it doesn't really stay on the condo anymore. It'll jump from, um, worrying about whether my cousin is secretly upset with me, to suddenly being so anxious that I'll make a mistake on a support call at work. And then before I know it, I'm stressing over how I'll ever save enough money or, you know, move forward with my life. \n\nI know everyone worries about their jobs and their future, so I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. I'm sure it's probably just regular life stuff. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this constant, low-level anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, let's see... I guess it's been a while now. Maybe around nine months? \n\nIt kind of started when, well, when the loan on my condo started moving toward foreclosure. I know that's a pretty stressful thing for anyone to go through, so I really thought the worry would just fade away once I got used to the situation, you know? But it just sort of stuck around. \n\nI really hope I'm not overreacting by still feeling this way after nine months. I mean, people deal with housing issues all the time and handle it way better than I am. Does nine months sound like a long time for this kind of thing, or is that pretty normal?\n\nDoctor: Has this constant feeling of anxiety been affecting your ability to do daily activities or enjoy things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I really try not to let it get in the way, but... well, at work, I've kind of been falling behind on my support tickets lately. I just get so worried about making a mistake that I end up double-checking even the really simple tasks over and over. I feel terrible about it because my coworkers have had to pick up some of the slack, and I hate being a burden to them. I'm sure they're probably annoyed with me, even if they don't say it.\n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I usually love going to dance nights and I do some mentoring, but I've actually skipped several of them recently. I just feel so... overwhelmed, I guess, that I can't bring myself to show up. \n\nI know I should just push through it, and I feel really guilty for dropping the ball on things. I'm trying to do better, I promise. Do you think it's really bad that it's affecting my work and my hobbies like this? I hope I don't sound like I'm just making excuses for being lazy.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety mostly focused on social situations, like what others think of you, or is it more about everyday life and tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess it's a little bit of both? But maybe more about just... everyday life stuff, mostly. \n\nLike I mentioned, I definitely worry about things like my condo and my support tickets at work, and just generally trying to keep my life on track. I mean, I do worry about what people think of me, too—like my cousin or my coworkers—but it's usually tied to me feeling like I'm messing up or not doing a good enough job, rather than just being in a social situation itself, if that makes sense? \n\nI really try to be a good friend and a good employee, so I just get so anxious that I'm letting people down. I hope that's a normal way to feel. Does that sound like what you were asking? I'm not totally sure if I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has your anxiety been affecting your relationships with family or friends?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little? But I really try hard not to let it. \n\nI mean, I don't want to be a downer or anything, so I try to keep it to myself when I'm around my friends or my cousin. I don't want them to think I'm, you know, constantly complaining or that I can't handle my own life. So I try to just put on a smile and be supportive of whatever they have going on. \n\nI guess I haven't been reaching out quite as much lately, just because I've been feeling so tired from worrying all the time. But I really hope they don't think I'm ignoring them! I'd hate for them to feel like I don't care about them. I'm sure I'm just overthinking it, right? I mean, everyone gets a little distant sometimes when they're stressed, don't they? I really hope I'm not being a bad friend.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're anxious in most areas of your life, or are there certain parts of your daily routine where you feel more at ease?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'd really love to say there are parts of my day where I just completely relax, because I know I should be able to do that. I try really hard to just focus on the positives. But, honestly, it feels like the worry is kind of... everywhere right now? I hope that doesn't sound too dramatic or anything. \n\nIt's just that, well, my worry doesn't really stay on just one thing, like the condo, anymore. It just sort of jumps around all day. Like, one minute I'll be worrying about whether my cousin is upset with me, and then suddenly I'm stressing over whether I'll make a mistake on a support call. And from there it just kind of snowballs into, you know, how I'll ever save money or just... move forward with my life in general. \n\nI really want to be the kind of person who can just leave work at work and feel at ease at home, but my brain just keeps bouncing from one worry to the next. I'm trying my best to keep it under control, I promise. Is it normal for it to jump around to so many different things like that? I hope I'm answering your question right, I'm sorry if I'm rambling.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety is causing you a lot of distress or is it more of a mild annoyance?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess I'd say it's... well, it's definitely more than just a mild annoyance. I really hate to admit this, because I always try to be so dependable, but... it's actually been causing me quite a bit of trouble lately. I hope you don't think less of me for saying that.\n\nIt's just that I've... I've started falling behind on my support tickets at work. I keep double-checking even the simplest tasks because I'm so terrified of messing up, and it takes me so long that my coworkers have had to pick up some of my slack. I feel so awful about it, I really want to pull my own weight, you know? \n\nAnd, um, outside of work... I've actually skipped a few of my dance nights recently, and even some of my mentoring sessions. I just... I get so overwhelmed by the end of the day that I can't bring myself to show up. I really try to be there for people, but I just feel so drained. Does that make sense? I'm really trying to push through it, I promise, but it's just been really hard to manage everything right now.\n\nDoctor: Is there any part of your life where you feel completely free from worry or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I'd really love to say yes. I mean, I try so hard to find those little moments of peace, you know? Like maybe when I'm just sitting on the couch with a cup of tea, I tell myself, \"Okay, just relax now.\" \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I hope this doesn't sound like I'm just not trying hard enough... I don't think there's anywhere that's totally free from it right now. Even when I'm supposed to be relaxing, my brain is just sort of humming in the background, reminding me of all the things I should be doing or worrying about. \n\nI really wish I could just switch it off, I promise I'm trying. Is it bad that I can't find a place where it just stops? I hope I'm not a lost cause or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself having repetitive thoughts or mental images that you can't stop thinking about, or feel compelled to perform specific rituals or behaviors?\n\nPatient: Um, no... I don't think I experience anything quite like that. It's not like a... a ritual or a specific routine I feel forced to do. \n\nAnd I don't really get strange mental images stuck in my head or anything. It's honestly just the regular worrying about everyday things, like I was saying before. Is... is that what you meant? I just want to make sure I'm understanding you right and giving you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: Is there any one area of your life where you feel your anxiety is significantly less than in others?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm really trying to think if there is. I want to give you a good answer, but... honestly, I don't think there's really any one area that's significantly better than the rest. \n\nI mean, I try my best to leave work stress at work and not bring it home, and I really do try to just enjoy my weekends... but the worry just kind of follows me everywhere, I guess. It's like a low-level buzz that's just always there, no matter what I'm doing. \n\nI'm sorry, I wish I could say there was a specific place where I felt totally fine. I hope that's an okay answer? I just don't want to make things sound better than they are, even though I'm really trying to work on it.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that you do or any situation where you consistently feel no anxiety at all?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm trying to be as helpful as I can... am I giving you the kind of information you need to figure this out?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble sleeping due to your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess my sleep hasn't been the greatest lately. I mean, I really do try to have good habits and get enough rest, I know how important that is! And even when I finally do fall asleep, I usually wake up feeling like I barely rested at all. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound like I'm just whining about being tired... is that what you were asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right details.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social interactions or events because of your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess, yeah, a little bit? I get to the end of the day and I feel so overwhelmed that I just can't bring myself to go. \n\nI'm really trying to get better about it, though!
